Next, I determine the first term (a₁). Since a₂ = a₁ + d, substituting the known values gives me 28 = a₁ + 8. Solving for a₁, I find that a₁ = 20.

With both the common difference and the first term established, I use the formula for the sum of the first n terms in an arithmetic sequence: Sₙ = (n/2) × [2a₁ + (n - 1)d].

Plugging in the values, where n is 102, a₁ is 20, and d is 8, I calculate each part step by step. 

First, compute 2a₁: 2 × 20 = 40.

Then, calculate (n - 1) × d: (102 - 1) × 8 = 101 × 8 = 808.

Adding these together gives me the value inside the brackets: 40 + 808 = 848.

Finally, multiplying by n and dividing by 2 yields the sum of the first 102 terms: (102/2) × 848 = 51 × 848. Multiplying these gives me a total sum of 43,248.

To find the sum of the first **44** terms of an arithmetic progression (AP), we can use the formula for the \(n\)-th term and the sum of the first \(n\) terms.

### Given:
- Common difference (\(d\)) = 14
- The \(44^{th}\) term (\(a_{44}\)) = 298

### Step-by-Step Solution:

1. **Find the First Term (\(a_1\)):**

   Using the formula for the \(n\)-th term of an AP:
   \[
   a_n = a_1 + (n - 1)d
   \]
   
   Plugging in the known values for the \(44^{th}\) term:
   \[
   298 = a_1 + (44 - 1) \times 14 \\
   298 = a_1 + 602 \\
   a_1 = 298 - 602 \\
   a_1 = -304
   \]

2. **Calculate the Sum of the First \(n\) Terms (\(S_n\)):**

   The formula for the sum of the first \(n\) terms of an AP is:
   \[
   S_n = \frac{n}{2} \times (a_1 + a_n)
   \]
   
   Plugging in the values:
   \[
   S_{44} = \frac{44}{2} \times (-304 + 298) \\
   S_{44} = 22 \times (-6) \\
   S_{44} = -132
   \]

### Final Answer:
\[
\boxed{-132}
\]

S_n = \frac{n}{2} [2a + (n-1)d]

To determine the common difference (d) of the arithmetic progression, I start by using the formula for the nth term of an arithmetic sequence:

aₙ = a₁ + (n - 1)d

Given:
- The 34th term is more than the 20th term by 14.
This translates to:
a₃₄ = a₂₀ + 14

Substituting the formula for each term:
a₁ + 33d = (a₁ + 19d) + 14

Simplifying the equation:
a₁ cancels out on both sides, leaving:
33d = 19d + 14

Next, I isolate d by subtracting 19d from both sides:
14d = 14

Finally, dividing both sides by 14 gives:
d = 1
</think>

**Solution:**

We need to find the common difference (\(d\)) of an arithmetic progression (A.P.) where the \(34^{th}\) term is greater than the \(20^{th}\) term by \(14\).

An arithmetic progression follows the general formula:
\[ a_n = a_1 + (n - 1)d \]
where:
- \(a_n\) is the \(n^{th}\) term,
- \(a_1\) is the first term,
- \(d\) is the common difference.

Given:
\[ a_{34} = a_{20} + 14 \]

Using the general formula for both terms:
\[
\begin{align*}
a_{34} &= a_1 + (34 - 1)d \\
&= a_1 + 33d
\end{align*}
\]
\[
\begin{align*}
a_{20} &= a_1 + (20 - 1)d \\
&= a_1 + 19d
\end{align*}
\]

Substituting \(a_{20}\) into the given relationship:
\[
a_1 + 33d = (a_1 + 19d) + 14
\]

Simplifying:
\[
33d - 19d = 14 \\
14d = 14 \\
d = \frac{14}{14} \\
d = 1
\]

**Final Answer:**
\[\boxed{1}\]

To find the quadratic equation whose roots are **16** and **6**, we can use Vieta's formulas. 

For a quadratic equation of the form:
\[ ax^2 + bx + c = 0 \]

- The sum of the roots (\( \alpha + \beta \)) is given by \( -\frac{b}{a} \).
- The product of the roots (\( \alpha \cdot \beta \)) is given by \( \frac{c}{a} \).

Given the roots are **16** and **6**, we have:
- Sum of roots: \( 16 + 6 = 22 \)
- Product of roots: \( 16 \times 6 = 96 \)

Therefore, the quadratic equation is:
\[ x^2 - (sum\,of\,roots)x + (product\,of\,roots) = 0 \]
Substituting the values:
\[ x^2 - 22x + 96 = 0 \]

**Final Answer:**
\[
\boxed{x^2 - 22x + 96 = 0}

Given that the final amount is 4.65 times the initial amount over a period of 4 years, we need to find the annual growth rate assuming compound interest.

We start with the formula for compound growth:
\[ A = P(1 + g)^t \]

Where:
- \( A \) is the final amount,
- \( P \) is the initial amount,
- \( g \) is the annual growth rate,
- \( t \) is the time period in years.

Given:
\[ A = 4.65P \]
\[ t = 4 \]

We need to solve for \( g \). Rearranging the formula, we get:
\[ (1 + g)^t = \frac{A}{P} \]
\[ (1 + g)^4 = 4.65 \]

Taking the fourth root of both sides:
\[ 1 + g = 4.65^{1/4} \]

Using logarithms to solve for \( g \):
\[ \ln(4.65) = 1.533 \]
\[ \frac{\ln(4.65)}{4} = \frac{1.533}{4} \approx 0.383 \]

Exponentiating both sides:
\[ e^{0.383} \approx 1.467 \]

Thus, we find:
\[ 1 + g \approx 1.467 \]
\[ g \approx 0.467 \]

Converting this to a percentage:
\[ g \approx 46.7\% \]

Therefore, the annual growth rate is approximately:
\[ \boxed{46.7\%} \]

First, I need to understand the problem. The population increases by 15% for two consecutive years and then decreases by 5% in the third year.

To find the average annual growth rate over these three years, I'll use the formula for compound growth:

Final Population = Initial Population × (1 + Growth Rate₁) × (1 + Growth Rate₂) × ... × (1 + Growth Rateₙ)

Let’s assume an initial population of 100.

After a 15% increase in the first year:
100 × 1.15 = 115

After another 15% increase in the second year:
115 × 1.15 = 132.25

After a 25% decrease in the third year:
132.25 × (1 - 0.25) = 99.1875

The final population after three years is approximately 99.1875.

To find the average growth rate, I set up the equation:

Final Population = Initial Population × (1 + Average Growth Rate)^3
99.1875 = 100 × (1 + x)^3

Solving for x:
(1 + x)^3 ≈ 0.991875
Taking the cube root of both sides:
1 + x ≈ ∛0.991875 ≈ 0.99726
x ≈ -0.00274 or -0.274%

The average annual growth rate is approximately -0.27%, indicating a slight decrease over the three years.
</think>

To determine the **average annual growth rate** for the population over three years with varying rates, we'll use the concept of compound growth.

### Given:
- **Year 1 Growth Rate**: \( 15\% \)
- **Year 2 Growth Rate**: \( 15\% \)
- **Year 3 Growth Rate**: \( -5\% \) (since it's a decrease)

Let’s assume an initial population of \( P_0 = 100 \).

#### Step 1: Calculate the Population After Each Year
1. **After Year 1**:
   \[
   P_1 = P_0 \times (1 + 0.15) = 100 \times 1.15 = 115
   \]
   
2. **After Year 2**:
   \[
   P_2 = P_1 \times (1 + 0.15) = 115 \times 1.15 = 132.25
   \]
   
3. **After Year 3**:
   \[
   P_3 = P_2 \times (1 - 0.05) = 132.25 \times 0.95 = 125.6375
   \]

#### Step 2: Calculate the Average Annual Growth Rate
The formula for average growth rate over \( n \) years is:
\[
\text{Average Growth Rate} = \left( \frac{P_n}{P_0} \right)^{\frac{1}{n}} - 1
\]

Here, \( P_n = 125.6375 \), \( P_0 = 100 \), and \( n = 3 \).

Plugging in the values:
\[
\text{Average Growth Rate} = \left( \frac{125.6375}{100} \right)^{\frac{1}{3}} - 1
\]
\[
= (1.256375)^{\frac{1}{3}} - 1
\]
\[
\approx 1.0789 - 1 = 0.0789 \text{ or } 7.89\%
\]

### Final Answer:
The average annual growth rate is approximately **7.89%**.

\boxed{\text{Average Annual Growth Rate: 7.89\%}}

First, I need to identify the first 10 prime numbers. Prime numbers are whole numbers greater than 1 that have no positive divisors other than 1 and themselves.

The first 10 prime numbers are:
2, 3, 5, 7, 11, 13, 17, 19, 23, and 29.

Next, I will calculate the sum of these prime numbers by adding them together:

10 + 7 = 17

28 + 13 = 41  
41 + 17 = 58  
58 + 19 = 77  
77 + 23 = 100  
100 + 29 = 129

So, the total sum of the first 10 prime numbers is 129.

Now, to find the arithmetic mean, I will divide this sum by the number of terms, which is 10:

Arithmetic Mean = Total Sum / Number of Terms  
Arithmetic Mean = 129 / 10  
Arithmetic Mean = 12.9

Therefore, the arithmetic mean of the first 10 prime numbers is 12.9.

***********************<think>
First, let's understand the problem. There are 40 numbers with an arithmetic mean of 38.

So, the total sum of these numbers is:
Sum = Number of terms × Mean = 40 × 38 = 1520

It was later discovered that one number was incorrectly recorded as 56 instead of its actual value, which should be 36. This means the incorrect entry overstates the sum by (56 - 36) = 20.

To find the correct total sum:
Correct Sum = Incorrect Sum - Difference
Correct Sum = 1520 - 20 = 1500

Finally, calculate the correct arithmetic mean:
Mean = Correct Sum / Number of terms = 1500 / 40 = 37.5
</think>

To determine the correct arithmetic mean after identifying the incorrect value, follow these steps:

**Step 1: Calculate the Incorrect Total Sum**
- **Number of terms (n):** 40
- **Incorrect Mean:** 38
- **Total sum with incorrect value (Sum₁):**
  
  \[
  \text{Sum}_1 = n \times \text{Mean} = 40 \times 38 = 1520
  \]

**Step 2: Identify the Difference Due to Incorrect Value**
- **Incorrectly recorded value:** 56
- **Correct value:** 36
- **Difference (Δ):**

  \[
  \Delta = 56 - 36 = 20
  \]

**Step 3: Calculate the Correct Total Sum**
  
  \[
  \text{Correct Sum} = \text{Sum}_1 - \Delta = 1520 - 20 = 1500
  \]

**Step 4: Determine the Correct Arithmetic Mean**

  \[
  \text{Mean} = \frac{\text{Correct Sum}}{n} = \frac{1500}{40} = 37.5
  \]

**Final Answer:**

\boxed{37.5}
